Task 4 + Task 5 · Spatial QA

This site contains only Task 4 multi-human reasoning and Task 5 gaze-grounded reasoning. Every answer is computed and signed before any optional language API call. Submit an answer first; localization, gaze/mask, and computed evidence remain collapsed until requested.

Task 4 · Seven requested categories

1 · Distance evolution
3 published case(s)
2 · Passing side + final position
1 published case(s)
3 · Dominant interaction relation
5 published case(s)
4 · Reunion relation restoration
1 published case(s)
5 · Cause of relation change
2 published case(s)
6 · Group reorganization
2 published case(s)
7 · Physical visibility/occlusion timeline
0 published · blocked: no physical blocker geometry

Task 5 · Three requested categories

Only metric object geometry in a validated wearer body frame counts below. Image-plane gaze/mask questions are auxiliary and do not satisfy these categories.
1 · Relation change between two gazes at X
1 published case(s)
2 · Body-side change when gaze turns to X
1 published case(s)
3 · Full-clip relation change of the last gaze-annotated object
1 published case(s)
Auxiliary evidence-closed gaze questions
0 published · not counted toward the three categories

ok

Task 4 · Multi-Human Relational Dynamics

dominant_facing_relation_over_video
Q1. What is the dominant body-facing relation between the two people in the video?
ok
Correct answer: C
Facing away from each other is the dominant relation for most of the clip.
Deterministic body-forward classification gives {'back_to_back_or_away': 16} across the 16-sample timeline.
Show computed evidence for this question
Projects both SMPL-X root-forward vectors and the the man in a dark navy short-sleeve T-shirt and light gray trousers↔the woman in a gray printed T-shirt and blue jeans direction onto the ground plane, then aggregates the facing state over all samples.

relation_change_cause
Q1. The interaction partner changes from right-front to left-front relative to the camera wearer. Is this change produced by their relative position movement, the camera wearer's body turn, or both?
ok
Correct answer: D
Both relative position movement and the camera wearer's body turn are required.
Two deterministic counterfactuals separately hold the starting orientation and starting positions fixed.
